8. F → G → E → L → M → J

- F cannot reach H, M, L, or any nodes beyond E
- The entire graph becomes disconnected at E, preventing any connection between F and J

Since E is present in every path between F and J, removing just E is sufficient to disconnect the two black nodes. No other single node removal would work, as other nodes appear in only some paths but not all.

Path 5: M → J → P → C → N → B → H
Path 6: M → J → P → O → Y → N → B → H

I can see that there are two main branches from M: one through E and one through J. To disconnect M and H, I need to block all paths.

- Removing E blocks paths 1, 2, 3 but leaves paths 4, 5, 6 intact
- Removing J blocks paths 4, 5, 6 but leaves paths 1, 2, 3 intact
- Removing any other single node still leaves multiple paths intact

Therefore, I need to remove at least two nodes. The most efficient approach is to remove one node from each branch:
- Removing E and J will block all paths: E blocks the E-branch paths, and J blocks the J-branch paths

Verification: After removing E and J, there are no remaining paths between M and H. All six paths are completely severed.

This is the minimum number since no single node can disconnect M and H, and two nodes are sufficient to block all paths.

Path 3: G → X → F → E → B → N → C → I
Path 4: G → K → X → F → E → B → C → I
Path 5: G → K → X → F → E → B → N → C → I

- The direct connection through H, or
- The longer paths through the lower portion of the graph (involving X, F, E, B, C, and N)

To disconnect G and I, I need to break all possible paths. If I remove only H, the direct path is broken, but all the other paths through the lower portion remain intact. If I remove only C, many paths are broken, but the direct path G → H → I still exists.

However, if I remove both H and C, I break all paths:
- Removing H eliminates the direct path G → H → I
- Removing C eliminates all paths that go through the lower portion (since C is a required node in all those paths)

This is the minimum number of nodes needed because:
1. No single node can disconnect G and I (as shown above)
2. The two nodes H and C are sufficient to break all paths between G and I

Therefore, the minimum cut set requires 2 nodes: H and C.

Final Answer: Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [H, C]
